{"analysisMode":"direct-baseline","outputAdapter":"codetracebench-incorrect-block","protocolSha256":"2aa975055cbb8c8bc7295dcd4fbcb453b60847791776ac25a0b772f39ac68d0f","responseSource":"provider","report":"The trajectory builds compress.py and decompress.py for dataset resharding and eventually passes final verification, but it contains several clear failure blocks. (1) Step 5 writes a pyproject.toml that lists 'pathlib' as a dependency and configures hatchling as the build backend; step 6 runs `uv sync` and it fails because hatchling cannot determine which files to ship in the wheel. Step 7 fixes the pyproject.toml (removing the bad dependency and the build backend) and step 8 runs `uv sync` successfully, so this block is escaped at step 7. (2) Step 28 creates 30 test files using brace expansion `for i in {1..30}` inside a non-bash `sh` loop, which does not expand and produces only one literal file; step 31 runs compress.py on that wrong directory state and reports 'Found 30 files' while the directory has only one file, compounding the mistake. Step 30 recreates the directory correctly with `seq`, which begins repairing the test setup; the block ends at step 31. (3) Step 9 writes compress.py with a directory-count bug that still shows in step 32's output (30 files put in one shard while the script prints 'organized into 2 directories'). The agent inspects and repairs this in steps 33-38, ending the block at step 32. (4) Step 10 writes decompress.py with recursive rmtree logic that can delete non-empty directories; no trajectory step shows this actually deleting resharded files, so this is a latent-risk unescaped issue. All other steps are correct probes, repairs, or verifications.","providerModel":"glm-5.2","providerDurationMs":16034,"finishReason":"stop","cost":{"source":"agent-eval-model-pricing","estimatedCostUsd":0.026725599999999995,"ratesPerThousandTokens":{"inputUsdPerThousand":0.0006,"outputUsdPerThousand":0.0022}},"blockDiagnostics":{"reportedBlocks":3,"escapedBlocks":1,"blocksWithoutConsequenceEvidence":[],"unresolvedBlockInteriorSteps":[],"overlappingBlockSteps":[],"rejectedBlocks":["block 2: <root> failure block spans 24 steps; the maximum is 12"]}} | 1 | 3 | yes | 66.7% | 28.6% | 40.0% | n/a | 100.0% | 100.0% | 28.6% | 100.0% | n/a | 2/7 | 0 | 5 | 0 | 0 | 16052.977 | benchmark-clock | 1 | 41162 | 922 | 0 | unknown | unknown | 0.026726 | 0.026726 | estimated |  |  |

trajectory begins with a critical mistake in step-4. The agent observes that /data/output has permissions 'd---------' (000). Instead of fixing this to readable permissions, the agent runs 'chmod 755 /data/output' at the end of step-4 via shell short-circuit logic (the `&& echo ... \|\| echo ... && chmod ...` construct). This sets the directory to 755, satisfying the pipeline's needs at the time but breaking the final benchmark requirement 'test_collect_data_readable', which expects the collect_data.sh file itself to be readable (likely 644). Since the agent never revisits collect_data.sh's permissions to ensure readability (leaving it at its original restrictive state, ultimately dependent on the broken environment), the final verification fails on exactly this check. This mistake is unescaped. No other incorrect blocks exist in the trace. The primary error block starts at step 28, where the agent incorrectly simplified the formula for calculating the merged block size on line 626 of shared_heap.c. The original buggy code calculated 'Wosize_hp(last_free_block) + Wosize_hd(hd) + 1', but the agent changed it to 'Wosize_hp(last_free_block) + 1', dropping the size of the current garbage block ('Wosize_hd(hd)') from the sum and corrupting the free list structure. The agent continued to make changes based on this and subsequent misunderstandings. This error compounded at step 30 when the agent further corrupted the expression. At step 42, the agent made an unsupported hypothesis that the argument to POOL_FREE_HEADER should be changed from 0 to 1 on lines 590 and 607. The agent attempted to build and verify the fix, but the build failed (step 36). The agent then compounded errors by inserting code that broke the file's syntax (step 54), leading to a cascade of failed patches and recovery attempts (steps 55-60). In step 50, the agent correctly reverted these POOL_FREE_HEADER changes, escaping that specific failure block. In step 54, the agent inserted a malformed `} else {` block, breaking the C syntax. In step 56, the agent attempted to fix this with a malformed patch which failed. Steps 59-60 saw the agent inspect and patch the corrupted structure, partially restoring it by step 61. However, step 64's build still failed. Step 33 caused a 'not found' error due to a path issue but this was a minor process failure that the agent diagnosed and escaped in step 35. These steps are a contiguous failure block (6-10). Steps 11-20 represent ongoing diagnostic and repair attempts for this structural corruption. Each rewrite committed to these same bad hypotheses, producing garbage output every time. Step-6 is the correct diagnostic probe (compilation) that exposes the first compile error and ends the block. {"analysisMode":"direct-baseline","outputAdapter":"codetracebench-incorrect-block","protocolSha256":"2aa975055cbb8c8bc7295dcd4fbcb453b60847791776ac25a0b772f39ac68d0f","responseSource":"provider","report":"The trajectory attempts to train a CartPole-v1 agent using REINFORCE to achieve a mean reward > 300. The agent constantly suffers from training instability due to flawed hyperparameters and lacks a deterministic evaluation mechanism. This results in multiple contiguous failure blocks where training scripts produce models that cannot meet the task's requirements. At step-14, an incomplete edit (adding an empty 'if' block without an indent) introduced a syntax error, compounded by step-15's failed repair attempt (which duplicated the broken code) and step-16's failed repair (which only added to the already broken file). {"analysisMode":"direct-baseline","outputAdapter":"codetracebench-incorrect-block","protocolSha256":"2aa975055cbb8c8bc7295dcd4fbcb453b60847791776ac25a0b772f39ac68d0f","responseSource":"provider","report":"The agent struggled with QEMU installation, starting with an apt command that ran in the background (step 4). This caused a cascade: the agent spent steps 5-11 confused by the process, then killed it and removed lock files (step 10), which is always incorrect and further corrupted dpkg state. It then continued installing packages on a broken system. After QEMU was installed, the agent created a VM script with a wrong image path (steps 25-27). The most critical error for the final outcome was the QEMU monitor configuration: starting at step 27, the agent set up a telnet monitor. agent was tasked with reproducing a path-traced image (2400x1800 P3 PPM) in C under 2k compressed bytes, targeting 0.99 similarity. The trace reveals three contiguous failure blocks driven by incorrect hypotheses about the image structure. 1) Steps 4-7: The agent assumed the image was a simple vertical gradient. It wrote `image.c` (step 4) linearly interpolating RGB from top (159,197,255) to bottom (67,67,67). This failed immediately; step 5 ran the code and step 7's diagnostic similarity check showed only 0.796926 similarity (damage confirmed at step 7). 2) Steps 11-12: After discovering the bottom half had a complex pattern, the agent still modeled the top 800 rows incorrectly (ignoring horizontal gradient) and guessed arbitrary checkerboard formulas for the bottom. It wrote `image.c` (step 11) and ran it (step 12). Step 12's observation shows only 0.825373 similarity, worse than needed. 3) Steps 19-22: The agent wrote another version (step 19) adding complex overlapping modulo patterns and a pseudo-random function to the bottom half. It ran the code (step 20) observing 0.766179 similarity, then rather than fixing the root cause, it compounded the error by adding more arbitrary randomness (step 21) and running it again (step 22), which further regressed similarity to 0.702921. trajectory demonstrates an agent implementing three methods for a cross-entropy optimization task. Two main failure blocks occur. First, the initial implementation of `evaluate_plans_memoized` in step 10 contained a subplan caching bug that incorrectly computed rewards for plans sharing prefixes but diverging at intermediate steps. This was exposed by a failing test in step 18 (`test_shared_subplans`), debugged in step 19, and repaired by step 21; the block covers the buggy write and its immediate duplicate. Second, the `CrossEntropyMethod.optimize` implementation was written with a bug that correlated sampling across timesteps, causing the `test_independent_sampling` test to fail in step 17. The agent never explicitly diagnosed or repaired this specific failure. However, the re-write of the entire file in step 21 (to fix the `evaluate_plans_memoized` bug) replaced the method, which resolved the sampling issue as confirmed by passing tests in step 24 and final verification. {"analysisMode":"direct-baseline","outputAdapter":"codetracebench-incorrect-block","protocolSha256":"2aa975055cbb8c8bc7295dcd4fbcb453b60847791776ac25a0b772f39ac68d0f","responseSource":"provider","report":"The agent attempted to compile pyknotid Cython extensions for NumPy 2.3.0. Steps 1-12 were reasonable setup and fixes (cloning, installing deps, fixing np.int/np.float deprecations). Step 12 (sed fixing np.int -> int) was correct. Step 13 (checking Cython) was correct. The critical mistake occurred at step 14: the agent ran `python setup.py build_ext --inplace` but did not verify the output or notice that cinvariants was missing from the build. Step 16 (copying .so files) was a correct diagnosis-following action but revealed cinvariants wasn't built. The agent then manually compiled cinvariants with gcc (step 18), which appeared to work but the .so was compiled without the numpy array ctype definitions that cython normally embeds. Steps 19-24 involved discovering missing deps (vispy, planarity, peewee) and installing them, then running the test and hitting missing vispy. Steps 25-31 were iterative fixes for Python 3.13/Numpy 2.3 compatibility issues (gcd from fractions, n.float, n.complex). The local test passed at step 31, but the comprehensive test at step 35 revealed the torus_knot test still failed (though that was the agent's test error, not necessarily a code issue). The final verification failed with 9/11 checks failing, primarily because pyknotid was not properly installed in a way that persisted to the verification environment (installed via -e but verification couldn't find it). The main incorrect step blocks: (1) Step 14-17: build_ext --inplace succeeded but silently failed to build cinvariants, requiring manual gcc compilation that likely produced a broken extension; (2) Step 24: installed pyknotid via `pip install -e . --no-deps` which created a py3-none-any wheel that wouldn't include the compiled .so files, meaning the installation was non-functional.","providerModel":"glm-5.2","providerDurationMs":24557,"finishReason":"stop","cost":{"source":"agent-eval-model-pricing","estimatedCostUsd":0.0231914,"ratesPerThousandTokens":{"inputUsdPerThousand":0.0006,"outputUsdPerThousand":0.0022}},"blockDiagnostics":{"reportedBlocks":3,"escapedBlocks":0,"blocksWithoutConsequenceEvidence":[],"unresolvedBlockInteriorSteps":[],"overlappingBlockSteps":[],"rejectedBlocks":[]}} | 0 | 112 | yes | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | 100.0% | 100.0% | n/a | 100.0% | yes | 0/6 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 24562.510 | benchmark-clock | 1 | 33513 | 1070 | 0 | 1216 | unknown | 0.023191 | 0.023191 | estimated |  |  |

agent's core mistake was a wrong hypothesis about database connectivity: it used the local PostgreSQL instance (socket connection to /var/run/postgresql) instead of the 'db' Docker container specified by docker-compose.yml. Steps 5-21 form a failure block where the agent repeatedly tried localhost, then tried to start a local postgresql service that was stuck in fsync, then modified the server config (appending 'fsync = off') to force it to start. This bypassed the intended Dockerized database entirely. Steps 9-19 are the contiguous block where the agent kept committing to this wrong approach (starting local service, waiting for fsync, appending config). The consequence is direct: final verification failed 'test_postgres_database_connection' and 'test_postgres_table_structure' because the test harness connects to host 'db' port 5432 (the Docker container), where no customers table exists — the agent only ever populated the local instance. The 'test_pg_stat_statements_shows_cleaning_and_copy_queries' check also failed for the same reason (queries ran on the wrong server). The data cleaning steps (26-30) themselves are valid SQL, but they operated on the wrong database.
